3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               

                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and

leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

6.4    Finite-volume Perron input
Proposition 6.13 (Finite-volume Perron input from two-scale landing). For every finite spatial volume,
the one-step matching transfer kernel relative to product Haar is continuous, symmetric, and strictly
positive. If MΛ is the finite number of one-link updates in a sweep, then
                                                          −1
                                          KL (x, y) ≥ ϵM
                                                       ⋆ ZΛ,L > 0.
                                                        Λ



The compact transfer operator has a simple maximal eigenvalue and a unique strictly positive Perron
eigenfunction. No volume-uniform lower bound for the complete kernel is asserted.
Proof. Apply Theorem 6.10 with one live pivot at a time. Corollary 6.11 gives the finite-volume product
lower bound. The aligned half-step is SL∗ SL , so the transfer is positive and self-adjoint. Jentzsch–Perron
applies in finite volume.

6.5    Exact Haar–optical matching law
The matching macroblock used after the compact first crossing is the exact pushforward by oriented
length-L path products. It is useful to distinguish the coordinates that define the map from the coordinates
used to estimate a particular retained coefficient.
   Fix one proof color. Its straight macro-paths are pairwise edge-disjoint and have mutually disjoint
buffered interiors. For one path γ = (e1 , . . . , eL ) define
                                            Pγ (U ) = Ue1 · · · UeL .
The retained macro-link is Wγ = Pγ (U ). For paths not carrying an active coefficient one may use the
standard coordinates
                            Wγ = Pγ (U ),      Zγ,k = Uek , 2 ≤ k ≤ L,
with
                                         Ue1 = Wγ (Zγ,2 · · · Zγ,L )−1 .
The full map BL is the finite composition of the colorwise pushforwards, with the reflection plane on
macro-cell boundaries.
Lemma 6.14 (Unit-Haar macroblock disintegration). For every path,
                                L

                                      dmG (Uek ) = dmG (Wγ )         dmG (Zγ,k ).
                                k=1                            k=2

Consequently the simultaneous path-product map has unit Haar Jacobian, is gauge covariant at path
endpoints, and intertwines aligned reflection.
Proof. For fixed details, Ue1 7→ Wγ is right multiplication by a fixed group element. Haar invariance
proves the identity. Edge-disjointness tensorizes it, gauge transformations telescope, and reflection reverses
and inverts the product.

renormalization matrix. Those matrices are treated in the local root norm; after the declared source
normalization their products are bounded on every fixed finite jet family and do not alter the connector
exponent.
Proof. Each coarse value is a convex average of the fine values, proving the first inequality. The second
follows by the triangle inequality and the partition of the fine cells into coarse cells.

Connectors at one scale The exact iterated conditional logarithm is divided into two propagation
regimes.
(R1) There is a fixed weak threshold jw such that every finer scale j ≥ jw lies in one strict rooted expansion
     ball. After distributing the support weight in the tree estimate, every additional nonreference vertex
     pays a common branch factor σ < 1. The exact Karcher detail covariance obeys the exponential
     scale-local bond of Corollary 4.14; fixed connector words have physical diameter O(ℓj ), and the
     weak/irregular recombination has a summable rooted remainder.
(R2) The finitely many scales between jw and the compact matching scale are integrated inside the finite
     macrobridge. Their source dependence is absorbed into bounded matching-scale local atoms; they
     are not counted as independent long-distance connectors.
(R3) At the matching scale, Theorem 6.43 gives the exact joint Haar–optical two-buffer connector, and
     Theorem 6.44 permits each buffer to contain an arbitrary fixed finite local source cluster, with
     common spacetime exponent mE > 0. Corollary 7.8 excludes an additional unreported massless
     acoustic reference channel beyond the exact owner-map inventory.
(R4) One or two bounded or normalized source buffers may remain as declared roots with row and
     column bounds uniform in all exterior variables.

Proof. Choose any root x ∈ S. The cone gives βS ≤ be−κτ (S) . Multiply by the cardinality bound and
take the scalar supremum.
Theorem 3.9 (Corrected support closure). Assume:
1. the summed replica-BKAR theorem;
2. the geometric reporting lemma;
3. the complete-species allocation cone;
4. the exact dimension-ledger reblocking factor 24−∆ ;
5. a logarithmic-support localization estimate
                           qloc (D, L) ≤ C L−3 + (log L)pD LΓ−δD ,
                                                               
                                                                                      δD → ∞.
Choose κ = µ/2. If the rooted regular atom mass and allocation radius are small enough that
                                                     χeff (u, r, b) < 1,
then one may choose finite D and finite L = 2s so that the stable regular derivative satisfies
                                                 ∥DΦreg |stable ∥ < 1
in the same permanent κ-weighted support norm.
Proof. The first two lemmas leave a residual connector decay µ − κ > 0 and convert every fixed geometric
and regulator cost into a per-vertex constant. The summed BKAR tree recursion is therefore geometric
in the effective rooted link mass χeff . Reblocking is paid by the engineering factor and does not weaken κ.
Choose D with δD > Γ + 3, then choose finite L so qloc lies inside the strict associated-graded jet gap.
Finally shrink u, r, b to absorb the nonlinear and irregular source terms.

restriction. Assume                           √
                                     ∥E+ ∥ ≤ 2,      ∥R+ ∥ ≤ 1,
and that every retained row in the positive schedule is supported in the positive closed half-space and
fixed reflection boundary.

                                               P+ = R+ P E+ .

                                                            49
Proof. Closed-half-space support gives L+ R+ = R+ L on reflected fields. Therefore

                                     L+ P+ = R+ LP E+ = R+ E+ = I.

The norm estimate is immediate. On the real Brillouin zone the bulk normal section satisfies ∥P ∥2 ≤ 8/49,
yielding the displayed sharper bound.

Lemma 3.5 (Charged support of every irregular label). After fixing 0 < χ ≤ χ0 with χ20 ≤ 4cch , every
curvature, collar, or outer label is supported on

                                                 AQ+ (U ) ≥ c0 χ2

for one fixed c0 > 0. Every derivative of a splice cutoff through order 2sG is supported on the same
charged event and has a fixed-template polynomial bound in χ−1 and the fixed chart thresholds.

Proof. On the support of 1 − γD , either DQ ≥ χ2 /4 or a derivative of γD is present. The retention
inequality gives AQ+ ≥ χ2 /(4Cret ). On the collar and outer supports, or on a derivative of γ0 or γ1 , one
has TQ ≥ tch /8 after decreasing the fixed thresholds once. The compactness argument in Theorem 3.4
gives a fixed positive action charge, which dominates c0 χ2 for χ ≤ χ0 . The derivative bounds through
order 2sG follow from the fixed smooth cutoffs, Lemma 3.1, and the finite cell atlas; differentiation changes
neither transition support nor action charge.

retention estimate depend, and put

and the enlarged-cell overlap graph admits a νov -coloring.
Proposition 3.6 (Component action charge). For every bad seed X and every configuration in its labelled
support,
                                                            |X|
                                        AI(X) (U ) ≥ c∗ χ2      .

The statement is uniform under the standing convention.
Proof. A color class contains a subfamily S ⊂ X of pairwise disjoint enlarged cells with |S| ≥ |X|/νov .
By Lemma 3.5, every selected cell carries c0 χ2 of faithful Wilson action. The selected action supports are
disjoint, so their charges add. Enlarging the fixed cores once ensures all selected plaquettes lie in I(X).
Rename the finite constant.

Theorem 3.9 (Corrected support closure). Assume:
1. the summed replica-BKAR theorem;
2. the geometric reporting lemma;
3. the complete-species allocation cone;

                                                     χeff (u, r, b) < 1,

                                                 ∥DΦreg |stable ∥ < 1
in the same permanent κ-weighted support norm.
Proof. The first two lemmas leave a residual connector decay µ − κ > 0 and convert every fixed geometric
and regulator cost into a per-vertex constant. The summed BKAR tree recursion is therefore geometric
in the effective rooted link mass χeff . Reblocking is paid by the engineering factor and does not weaken κ.
Choose D with δD > Γ + 3, then choose finite L so qloc lies inside the strict associated-graded jet gap.
Finally shrink u, r, b to absorb the nonlinear and irregular source terms.

Theorem 3.9 (Corrected support closure). Assume:
1. the summed replica-BKAR theorem;
2. the geometric reporting lemma;
3. the complete-species allocation cone;
4. the exact dimension-ledger reblocking factor 24−∆ ;
5. a logarithmic-support localization estimate
                           qloc (D, L) ≤ C L−3 + (log L)pD LΓ−δD ,
                                                               
                                                                                      δD → ∞.
Choose κ = µ/2. If the rooted regular atom mass and allocation radius are small enough that
                                                     χeff (u, r, b) < 1,
then one may choose finite D and finite L = 2s so that the stable regular derivative satisfies
                                                 ∥DΦreg |stable ∥ < 1
in the same permanent κ-weighted support norm.
Proof. The first two lemmas leave a residual connector decay µ − κ > 0 and convert every fixed geometric
and regulator cost into a per-vertex constant. The summed BKAR tree recursion is therefore geometric
in the effective rooted link mass χeff . Reblocking is paid by the engineering factor and does not weaken κ.
Choose D with δD > Γ + 3, then choose finite L so qloc lies inside the strict associated-graded jet gap.
Finally shrink u, r, b to absorb the nonlinear and irregular source terms.

Lemma 3.12 (Finite word transport). There are fixed radii δout , δin > 0 and a constant Ctr < ∞,
uniform under the standing convention, such that the component anchor solve is holomorphic for

                                       η ∈ WRX ,        h ∈ Θδout ,RX ,

and every Wilson word in the owned core atlas obeys
                               X
                                    ∥δAℓ (h)∥2 ≤ Ctr u qRX (h) + |X| .
                                                                    

                                ℓ∈I(X)

Its derivatives through order three have the same rooted local incidence bound.
Proof. Every owned plaquette is a finite word in the fixed forty-nine-path atlas. On the smaller tube, the
exponential, logarithm, Karcher solve, and principal-submatrix anchor inverse are holomorphic. Their
derivatives are bounded on the compact closure of each local template tube. There are only finitely many
bulk and aligned-reflection local templates; periodic wrapping does not change a rooted local template.
Summing the finite local row/column incidence bounds gives the displayed estimate.

                                                 1

                                                             1

Proof. The Wick martingale eliminates every covariance-contraction lowering term. Lemma 4.16 gives

   The extraction projector removes every declared jet below the stable threshold. Hence the stable matrix
is block upper triangular with the displayed diagonal. Its entries come from finitely many derivatives of
finite local analytic maps, so they are finite uniform constants. The spectrum of a finite block triangular
matrix is the union of the spectra of its diagonal blocks.

4.5    Localization and exact collect
Fix one finite dyadic macrostep L = 2s . All fields are expressed in the scale-dependent Wick coordinates
of the stable-jet theorem. Engineering degree includes discrete derivatives and every declared active
structural mark.

Rooted histories and collect. Let HL be the absolutely summable family of connected regular
macrostep histories. A history h has a reported connected support P (h), a distinguished root, and a
canonical hull
                                             Y = H(h).
The hull contains the reported supports, shortest covariance connector paths, and the fixed finite atlas
collar.
Definition 4.18 (Exact collect). For a coarse connected support Y , define
                                                     X
                                        (CL F )Y =         Fh .
                                                       h∈HL
                                                      H(h)=Y

The history certificate is retained as a passive label until this sum is formed.
Lemma 4.19 (Collect is a rooted partition). The sets

                                              {h : H(h) = Y }

partition HL . Hence collect is exact, and for every bounded linear localization operator AY ,
                                                       X
                                       AY (CL F )Y =        AY Fh .
                                                      H(h)=Y

No history is omitted or counted twice.
Proof. The canonical hull and history certificate are deterministic functions of the reported history.
Absolute convergence in the rooted activity norm permits bounded linear operations to pass through the
history sum.

Gauge-covariant local jet projector. For a rooted support Y , choose the canonical root cell r(Y ).
Parallel-transport every local tensor to r(Y ) along the canonical hull tree and write the resulting local
field in the finite normal-coordinate atlas.
    Let JD (Y ) be the finite-dimensional space of gauge-complete, signed-hypercubic covariant, Wick-local
jets of declared engineering degree at most D, including the extraction sector and the stable jet sector.
Lemma 4.20 (Discrete covariant localization). There is a projector

                                          LocD,Y : FY 7−→ JD (Y )

with the following properties.
1. Loc2D,Y = LocD,Y , and it is exact on JD (Y ).
2. It commutes with gauge transformations, signed-hypercubic symmetries, and aligned reflection.
3. For 0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
                                ∥ LocD,Y FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD ∥FY ∥[m] .
4. If LocD,Y FY = 0, then after canonical scale-L rescaling,

                                 ∥TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

  The estimate includes both the nonlocal output and its newly generated local projection:

                ∥ LocD TL FY ∥[m] + ∥(1 − LocD )TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

Proof. On each finite local template, take the gauge-complete Taylor expansion in normal coordinates
and scale-dependent Wick monomials, transport all coefficients to the canonical root, and average over
the finite stabilizer. The dual coefficient functionals use finitely many derivatives and finite-difference


                                                     32
moments. Transport along a tree of radius τ (Y ) costs at most a polynomial in 1 + τ (Y ), proving the
third row.
   If LocD,Y FY = 0, every declared Taylor coefficient through degree D vanishes. The integral form of
the covariant Taylor remainder begins at degree D + 1. Canonical rescaling gives one root-preimage factor
L4 and L−(D+1) from the smallest omitted engineering degree. Thus the gain is

                                              L4−(D+1) = L3−D .

The regular linearized block kernel is analytic on the fixed tube and bounded through the three active
marked rows, so integration and the local coefficient functionals preserve this remainder estimate. Applying
the projector after integration proves the displayed localization-commutator bound; in particular, a
remainder cannot feed the finite local block without paying the same L3−D factor.

Exact local block and localization commutator. Let ΠD denote the full local jet projector on the
                                  [s]
macrostep activity space and let Ljet be the exact finite stable Wick-jet matrix of Theorem 4.17.
Lemma 4.21 (Exact block decomposition). At the Gaussian point,

                                           DΦL (0)|stable = JL + NL ,

where
                                                                       [s]
                                          JL = ΠD DΦL (0)ΠD = Ljet ,
and every other block,

            ΠD DΦL (0)(1 − ΠD ),          (1 − ΠD )DΦL (0)ΠD ,           (1 − ΠD )DΦL (0)(1 − ΠD ),

is assigned to NL and obeys the long/short estimates below.
   In particular, the macrostep envelope LΓ does not multiply the finite local diagonal.
Proof. On the finite local Wick-jet quotient, Gaussian integration is exact second quantization and
                                         [s]
canonical rescaling gives the matrix Ljet proved in Theorem 4.17. This is the local-to-local block.
   For every coordinate containing a localization remainder, the preceding lemma gives the L3−D gain on
short supports. Local jets that produce degree above D also lie in the same remainder because the exact
forty-nine-path symbol raises degree only by even positive amounts. Thus all off-diagonal local/remainder
blocks belong to NL , while the finite local diagonal is computed before the support-envelope estimate.

Logarithmic support dichotomy. Assume the exact summed regular macrostep envelope
                                        [m]
                           X
                       sup   eκτL (Y ) cL (Y ) ≤ Cm LΓ , 0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
                              b
                                   Y ∋b

where τL is the rooted coarse tree radius.
  Choose
                                                   Γ+3
                                                           
                                            R(L) =     log L .
                                                    κ
Theorem 4.22 (Exact Loc/collect dichotomy). For every 0 ≤ m ≤ 3, the nonlocal block NL splits exactly
into long and short rows:
                                      NL = NLlong + NLshort .
  The long row satisfies
                                             ∥NLlong ∥[m] ≤ Cm L−3 .

  The short row satisfies

                                  ∥NLshort ∥[m] ≤ CD,m (1 + R(L))pD LΓ−(D−3) .

  Equivalently,
                                              δD = D − 3 −→ ∞.
   The estimates are uniform in periodic volume and preceding weak depth. They remain valid with one
or two passive exterior roots and every fixed finite passive source order.

Proof. For the long sector,
                                              [m]                                               [m]

   For the short sector, first apply exact collect and then the local projector. Linearity and absolute

                                              τL (Y ) ≤ R(L),

per collected activity. Summing the rooted envelope contributes LΓ , proving the short bound.
   The canonical root and history certificate are selected once, so neither hull reassignment nor collect

atlas.

Boundaries and aligned reflection. Use the corrected reflection schedule:
• block the two closed half-spaces separately;
• retain the reflection-plane link layer as fixed boundary data;
• use only path templates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.

                                              L4−(D+1) = L3−D .

The regular linearized block kernel is analytic on the fixed tube and bounded through the three active
marked rows, so integration and the local coefficient functionals preserve this remainder estimate. Applying
the projector after integration proves the displayed localization-commutator bound; in particular, a
remainder cannot feed the finite local block without paying the same L3−D factor.

Exact local block and localization commutator. Let ΠD denote the full local jet projector on the
                                  [s]
macrostep activity space and let Ljet be the exact finite stable Wick-jet matrix of Theorem 4.17.
Lemma 4.21 (Exact block decomposition). At the Gaussian point,

                                           DΦL (0)|stable = JL + NL ,

where
                                                                       [s]
                                          JL = ΠD DΦL (0)ΠD = Ljet ,
and every other block,

            ΠD DΦL (0)(1 − ΠD ),          (1 − ΠD )DΦL (0)ΠD ,           (1 − ΠD )DΦL (0)(1 − ΠD ),

is assigned to NL and obeys the long/short estimates below.
   In particular, the macrostep envelope LΓ does not multiply the finite local diagonal.
Proof. On the finite local Wick-jet quotient, Gaussian integration is exact second quantization and
                                         [s]
canonical rescaling gives the matrix Ljet proved in Theorem 4.17. This is the local-to-local block.
   For every coordinate containing a localization remainder, the preceding lemma gives the L3−D gain on
short supports. Local jets that produce degree above D also lie in the same remainder because the exact
forty-nine-path symbol raises degree only by even positive amounts. Thus all off-diagonal local/remainder
blocks belong to NL , while the finite local diagonal is computed before the support-envelope estimate.

Theorem 4.22 (Exact Loc/collect dichotomy). For every 0 ≤ m ≤ 3, the nonlocal block NL splits exactly
into long and short rows:
                                      NL = NLlong + NLshort .
  The long row satisfies
                                             ∥NLlong ∥[m] ≤ Cm L−3 .

  The short row satisfies

                                  ∥NLshort ∥[m] ≤ CD,m (1 + R(L))pD LΓ−(D−3) .

  Equivalently,
                                              δD = D − 3 −→ ∞.
   The estimates are uniform in periodic volume and preceding weak depth. They remain valid with one
or two passive exterior roots and every fixed finite passive source order.


                                                        33
Proof. For the long sector,
                                              [m]                                               [m]
                                   X                                            X
                         sup                  cL (Y ) ≤ e−κR(L) sup                    eκτL (Y ) cL (Y )
                          b                                                b
                                    Y ∋b                                        Y ∋b
                               τL (Y )>R(L)

                                                        ≤ Cm LΓ e−κR(L) ≤ Cm L−3 .

   For the short sector, first apply exact collect and then the local projector. Linearity and absolute
convergence make this identical to localizing each history and collecting by its canonical hull. Every
stable remainder lies in the kernel of LocD , while every off-diagonal local/remainder block has the same
Taylor-remainder gain. Since
                                              τL (Y ) ≤ R(L),
the discrete covariant localization lemma gives

                                              CD,m (1 + R(L))pD L3−D

per collected activity. Summing the rooted envelope contributes LΓ , proving the short bound.
   The canonical root and history certificate are selected once, so neither hull reassignment nor collect
produces a volume factor. Uniformity follows from the rooted envelope and the finite local template
atlas.

Boundaries and aligned reflection. Use the corrected reflection schedule:
• block the two closed half-spaces separately;
• retain the reflection-plane link layer as fixed boundary data;
• use only path templates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

Rooted histories and collect. Let HL be the absolutely summable family of connected regular
macrostep histories. A history h has a reported connected support P (h), a distinguished root, and a
canonical hull
                                             Y = H(h).
The hull contains the reported supports, shortest covariance connector paths, and the fixed finite atlas
collar.
Definition 4.18 (Exact collect). For a coarse connected support Y , define
                                                     X
                                        (CL F )Y =         Fh .
                                                       h∈HL
                                                      H(h)=Y

The history certificate is retained as a passive label until this sum is formed.
Lemma 4.19 (Collect is a rooted partition). The sets

                                              {h : H(h) = Y }

partition HL . Hence collect is exact, and for every bounded linear localization operator AY ,
                                                       X
                                       AY (CL F )Y =        AY Fh .

No history is omitted or counted twice.
Proof. The canonical hull and history certificate are deterministic functions of the reported history.
Absolute convergence in the rooted activity norm permits bounded linear operations to pass through the
history sum.

Gauge-covariant local jet projector. For a rooted support Y , choose the canonical root cell r(Y ).
Parallel-transport every local tensor to r(Y ) along the canonical hull tree and write the resulting local
field in the finite normal-coordinate atlas.
    Let JD (Y ) be the finite-dimensional space of gauge-complete, signed-hypercubic covariant, Wick-local
jets of declared engineering degree at most D, including the extraction sector and the stable jet sector.
Lemma 4.20 (Discrete covariant localization). There is a projector

                                          LocD,Y : FY 7−→ JD (Y )

with the following properties.
1. Loc2D,Y = LocD,Y , and it is exact on JD (Y ).
2. It commutes with gauge transformations, signed-hypercubic symmetries, and aligned reflection.
3. For 0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
                                ∥ LocD,Y FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD ∥FY ∥[m] .
4. If LocD,Y FY = 0, then after canonical scale-L rescaling,

                                 ∥TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

  The estimate includes both the nonlocal output and its newly generated local projection:

                ∥ LocD TL FY ∥[m] + ∥(1 − LocD )TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

Proof. On each finite local template, take the gauge-complete Taylor expansion in normal coordinates
and scale-dependent Wick monomials, transport all coefficients to the canonical root, and average over
the finite stabilizer. The dual coefficient functionals use finitely many derivatives and finite-difference


                                                     32
moments. Transport along a tree of radius τ (Y ) costs at most a polynomial in 1 + τ (Y ), proving the
third row.
   If LocD,Y FY = 0, every declared Taylor coefficient through degree D vanishes. The integral form of
the covariant Taylor remainder begins at degree D + 1. Canonical rescaling gives one root-preimage factor
L4 and L−(D+1) from the smallest omitted engineering degree. Thus the gain is

                                              L4−(D+1) = L3−D .

The regular linearized block kernel is analytic on the fixed tube and bounded through the three active
marked rows, so integration and the local coefficient functionals preserve this remainder estimate. Applying
the projector after integration proves the displayed localization-commutator bound; in particular, a
remainder cannot feed the finite local block without paying the same L3−D factor.

be the complete-species regulator-allocation cone.

The six one-step maps.          The exact regular step is organized in the following order.
(E1) Normal chart return. Substitute the finite-volume analytic normal section into the local Wilson,
     Haar, inherited, and source factors.


                                                              34
(E2) Coarea logarithm. Append the field-dependent logarithm

                                                  − log det(I + DN P ),

     with the fixed linear determinant owned once outside the activity map.
(E3) Summed connected logarithm. Apply the simultaneous hard-core/Gaussian replica-BKAR
     identity and sum the exact 1/n! series.
(E4) Local projection. Extract the complete gauge-covariant Wick-local jet and retain the short Taylor
     remainder.
(E5) Canonical hull and collect. Report covariance paths and finite collars, assign each history to
     one canonical hull, and collect exactly once.
(E6) Output recertification. Derive the next regulator allocation from the actual post-localization
     output gradient and complex Hessian.
   For 0 ≤ m ≤ 3, denote the corresponding derivative constants by
                             chart       coa       BKAR        loc      hull      rec
                            Km     ,    Km   ,    Km    ,     Km   ,   Km    ,   Km   .

Permanent support exponent.
Lemma 4.24 (One common support exponent). Suppose the covariance connector has decay exponent
µ > 0, and the normal chart, inverse trees, coarea logarithm, and fixed local atlas have positive exponential
locality exponents
                                      mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas > 0.
Choose
                                0 < κ < min{µ, mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas }.
Then all six maps act in the same κ-weighted rooted support space. No per-step weakening of κ is required.

Proof. For a BKAR tree, report every covariance edge by a shortest path and every hard-core edge by its
fixed collar. The geometric reporting inequality gives
                                        X           X
                               τ (P ) ≤   τ (Si ) +    de + Cgeo (n − 1).
                                           i            e∈Tcov


Multiplication by eκτ (P ) leaves the residual connector decay e−(µ−κ)de . Every fixed chart, inverse, coarea,
and atlas branch is summable at exponent κ. Localization costs a polynomial in the rooted radius but
does not alter the exponent. Canonical collect is a partition of histories, so it does not duplicate a support
weight.

Concrete connected-logarithm admission. Fix 0 < ϵ < 1/6 and use the physical weak-field radius

                                                 ∥A∥phys ≤ u1/2−ϵ .

A structural derivative is always taken in the scale-normalized metric: for an r-linear derivative, each
direction Hi is required to satisfy

                                       ∥Hi ∥phys ≤ u1/2−ϵ ,       1 ≤ i ≤ r.                            (SN)

Thus Dstr r
            F means the operator norm after the substitution Hi = u1/2−ϵ H       b i with ∥H
                                                                                           b i ∥phys ≤ 1. No

Lemma 4.25 (One common analytic species tube). There is a radius ηcom > 0, independent of volume
and preceding weak depth, on which the Wilson word, exponential-coordinate Haar density, Karcher
normal return, coarea logarithm, and finite Schur endpoint are simultaneously holomorphic. On the closed
half-radius tube, their derivatives through structural order three and every fixed passive-source order have
finite fixed-template suprema.

Proof. Each species is a finite composition of multiplication, inversion, exponential, logarithm, the
finite-cell Karcher mean, and an inverse of one of the matrices

                                         I + DN P,          Hopt + B(A).


                                                        35
The path-atlas theorem supplies a positive holomorphy radius for the first five maps. The uniform
inequalities ∥DN P ∥ < 1 and Hopt ≥ 2I persist on a smaller positive tube, so the two inverses are
holomorphic there by a Neumann series. There are only finitely many bulk and aligned-reflection
templates. Taking the minimum of their radii gives ηcom > 0; Cauchy’s estimate on the closed half-radius
tube gives the uniform derivative constants.
   For each fixed local template define the finite constants CW , CHaar , Ccoa , Cchart , CSch as the suprema
on that closed common tube of the third derivatives of respectively the Wilson word, Haar logarithm,
coarea logarithm, normal return, and finite Schur endpoint after their scalar and complete two-jet parts
have been removed. Let Cinc be the largest rooted incidence number of those templates and let Cconn be
the row sum of the exponential covariance connector of Corollary 4.14. All these constants are finite and
uniform under the standing convention.

                         1                                              3
                  ωW =     − 3ϵ > 0,      ωcoa = ωchart = ωSch =          − 3ϵ,      ω0 = ωW .
                         2                                              2

                                          ηinh ≤ Cinc r,     ηalloc ≤ Cinc b.                            (6)

Consequently
                                    ηatom (u, r, b) ≤ Catom (uω0 + r + b),
with the same estimate after three structural marks and every fixed passive source order.

   For the short sector, first apply exact collect and then the local projector. Linearity and absolute

per collected activity. Summing the rooted envelope contributes LΓ , proving the short bound.
   The canonical root and history certificate are selected once, so neither hull reassignment nor collect
produces a volume factor. Uniformity follows from the rooted envelope and the finite local template
atlas.

Boundaries and aligned reflection. Use the corrected reflection schedule:
• block the two closed half-spaces separately;
• retain the reflection-plane link layer as fixed boundary data;
• use only path templates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

pays the labelled-tree degree factorials. After the fixed support and Cauchy costs, every parent-normalized
child sum is bounded by Ctree ηatom ≤ 1/2. The connected tree series and its first three marked derivatives

formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

5       Invariant graph and universal coefficient
The marginal coordinate is u = g 2 . Inputs SI–6, SI–6B, SI–7, and SI–7M are used only to compute
the local perturbative Taylor coefficient of the complete fine Wilson vector-minus-ghost quotient. The
coefficient of the exact Karcher map is identified internally by evaluating one local standard background
coordinate on the two equal measures related by the exact Karcher pushforward, followed by unit-tangent
conjugacy on the centered invariant family. No unrestricted Haar-translated background functional, global
background-gauge section, or application of Reisz power counting to the optical conditional covariance is
consumed.

After the two background marks are taken, every finite-support background/quantum/ghost kernel of

Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A


                                                             44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN ) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

                       vertex family                 νIR       number of dimension-one legs    ρ

The two background marks create external legs and do not differentiate external momentum, so they do
not lower these orders. A mixed forest contraction is loop-free. A pure-Wilson contraction produces only
a standard dimension-four background-field vertex, while every quotient that retains the stable insertion
retains its degree-six marked vertex.
   Consequently every subtraction-generated exceptional configuration is covered by the SI–7M forest

                                   ω vector bubble = 4 − 2 · 2 + 1 + 1 = 2,

                                  ω seagull/tadpole = 4 − 2 = 2.

These values also cover the exceptional zero-momentum configurations created inside the Lowenstein
operators. A loop-bearing marked quotient has at least two additional infrared degrees. Thus every complete
packet has strictly positive lower infrared degree in every soft subspace.

Corollary 6.19 (Definition, exactness, and coefficientwise use of BL ). The matching macroblock BL is
a well-defined finite exact pushforward on the compact first-crossing class. For every Fourier, source-

the reference of Lemma 6.18; gauge covariance, reflection, and dyadic composition remain properties of
the underlying exact pushforward.
6.5.1 Compact collect with optical field live
The matching theorem is now proved on one exact joint law, before the normal/optical variables are
marginalized. This is the decisive ordering. The compact retained variables are reduced conditionally by
Haar–pivot integration; the resulting activities remain functions of the complete optical field; and only
then is the connected logarithm taken by the marked Gaussian forest theorem. Thus no retained density,
latent mixture, or residual-only gas is inserted between the physical law and the connected source kernel.

6.5.2 One exact joint matching law

Proof. The theorem concerns only the physical g-marked family; no Fréchet claim is made for arbitrary C 2

owners and certificates. This gives an exact marked representative of the derivative of the total compact

without changing operator ordering.

length. With these reselected values retain the strict margin (103). In the marked recursion, the only
unknown side sum at the current total g-mark order is transport through otherwise unmarked factors,
with coefficient exactly qsp < 1. An analytic hit uses the rows just proved; a freshly collected irregular hit
is a finite forcing activity handled by Lemma A.19. All remaining side factors have strictly lower marked

                     Bm ≤ qsp Bm + Pm (B0 , . . . , Bm−1 ) + Cm,Q ΛN,m ,         m = 1, 2,

and (234) follows without changing the unmarked admission coefficient.
   Finally apply the summed replica–BKAR logarithm to the exact post-compact identity, keeping the
two roots and every completed physical g mark distinguished. The unmarked branch mass stays below
its strict threshold; marked objects are finite forcing vertices. The complete canonical reported hull, not
a selected connector, carries the remaining exponential weight. This proves (235); (236) is its immediate
corollary.

6.4    Finite-volume Perron input
Proposition 6.13 (Finite-volume Perron input from two-scale landing). For every finite spatial volume,
the one-step matching transfer kernel relative to product Haar is continuous, symmetric, and strictly
positive. If MΛ is the finite number of one-link updates in a sweep, then
                                                          −1
                                          KL (x, y) ≥ ϵM
                                                       ⋆ ZΛ,L > 0.
                                                        Λ



The compact transfer operator has a simple maximal eigenvalue and a unique strictly positive Perron
eigenfunction. No volume-uniform lower bound for the complete kernel is asserted.
Proof. Apply Theorem 6.10 with one live pivot at a time. Corollary 6.11 gives the finite-volume product
lower bound. The aligned half-step is SL∗ SL , so the transfer is positive and self-adjoint. Jentzsch–Perron
applies in finite volume.

6.5    Exact Haar–optical matching law
The matching macroblock used after the compact first crossing is the exact pushforward by oriented
length-L path products. It is useful to distinguish the coordinates that define the map from the coordinates
used to estimate a particular retained coefficient.
   Fix one proof color. Its straight macro-paths are pairwise edge-disjoint and have mutually disjoint
buffered interiors. For one path γ = (e1 , . . . , eL ) define
                                            Pγ (U ) = Ue1 · · · UeL .
The retained macro-link is Wγ = Pγ (U ). For paths not carrying an active coefficient one may use the
standard coordinates
                            Wγ = Pγ (U ),      Zγ,k = Uek , 2 ≤ k ≤ L,
with
                                         Ue1 = Wγ (Zγ,2 · · · Zγ,L )−1 .
The full map BL is the finite composition of the colorwise pushforwards, with the reflection plane on
macro-cell boundaries.
Lemma 6.14 (Unit-Haar macroblock disintegration). For every path,
                                L
                                Y                              L
                                                               Y
                                      dmG (Uek ) = dmG (Wγ )         dmG (Zγ,k ).
                                k=1                            k=2

Consequently the simultaneous path-product map has unit Haar Jacobian, is gauge covariant at path
endpoints, and intertwines aligned reflection.
Proof. For fixed details, Ue1 7→ Wγ is right multiplication by a fixed group element. Haar invariance
proves the identity. Edge-disjointness tensorizes it, gauge transformations telescope, and reflection reverses
and inverts the product.
Definition 6.15 (Carrier-adapted hybrid chart). Let A be the finite set of retained macro-links carrying
nontrivial Peter–Weyl labels, bounded probes, or retained-source derivatives in the coefficient under study.
On every path γ ∈ A retain the original link coordinates
                                         Xγ,k = Uek ,        1 ≤ k ≤ L,
and regard Wγ = Pγ (X) only as the output map. On paths γ ∈        / A use the standard (Wγ , Zγ,2:L )
coordinates. Choose live pivots only among the X variables on active paths.

Lemma 6.17 (Carrier incidence and original-support locality). Let p be a live pivot on an active path γ.
With all other active path links fixed,
                                        Wγ = Bp Xp±1 Cp ,                                           (51)

                                          τ (Y ) ≥ cp R(k − 1).                                     (52)
Anchor reconstruction on an inactive path cannot alter the activity’s live pivot incidence.
Proof. Equation (51) is the ordered product split before and after p. The second statement follows by
joining the k pivot buffers inside the connected reported support. Every inactive-path coordinate is a
function only of links on that inactive path; because no live pivot lies there, its substitution cannot create
dependence on a live pivot.

Corollary 6.19 (Definition, exactness, and coefficientwise use of BL ). The matching macroblock BL is
a well-defined finite exact pushforward on the compact first-crossing class. For every Fourier, source-
marked, or normalized-probe coefficient of its retained density, the active paths may be analyzed in the
carrier-adapted hybrid chart without changing that coefficient. The charged pivot expansion uses precisely
the reference of Lemma 6.18; gauge covariance, reflection, and dyadic composition remain properties of
the underlying exact pushforward.
6.5.1 Compact collect with optical field live
The matching theorem is now proved on one exact joint law, before the normal/optical variables are
marginalized. This is the decisive ordering. The compact retained variables are reduced conditionally by
Haar–pivot integration; the resulting activities remain functions of the complete optical field; and only
then is the connected logarithm taken by the marked Gaussian forest theorem. Thus no retained density,
latent mixture, or residual-only gas is inserted between the physical law and the connected source kernel.



                                                       59
6.5.2 One exact joint matching law
Let W denote the compact retained variables in the exact Karcher chart and let ζ denote its complete
optical normal field. At finite volume and with physical source J, Theorem 4.13, Theorem 3.28, and the
exact owner map give
                              dµJ (W, ζ) = Z(J)−1 dγopt (ζ) dH(W ) e−VJ (W,ζ) .                   (53)
Here dH(W ) is product normalized Haar on the compact retained links. Indeed, ρ0,j in Theorem 4.13 owns
only the field-independent linear Jacobian and empty-support normalization; Proposition 6.8 absorbs that
scalar into Z(J) and restores product Haar. Every nonempty retained reference factor remains explicit in
VJ . Every nonempty Haar, coarea, chart, determinant, Schur, regular, irregular, seam, wrapping, and
source occurrence belongs to exactly one factor of VJ . Every scalar depending on an unintegrated variable
remains inside (53). Define only the complete normalized source logarithm

                                          W(J) = log Z(J) − log Z(0).                                   (54)

No marginal density is logarithmized.

Lemma 6.25 (Complete compact-flux classification and canonical exploration). Fix ζ and take the
Peter–Weyl decomposition of the complete compact incidence network generated by the exact pushforward
under BL . Integrate all compact variables coefficientwise. A connected history is compact-vacuum only
when every compact cut separating distinct mixed optical/source vertices carries the trivial representation.
After removal of already reported physical cross-core connectors, such a history factorizes into one-core
expectations or empty-support scalars and is assigned by the exact owner map to the normalized one-core
references or the pre-existing regular/Gaussian inventory.
   Every remaining history, including a multi-leg singlet with trivial visible coarse label but nontrivial
internal flux, has a deterministic visited-set exploration. Order incident half-edges before marking an
unoriented edge inspected. Discovery edges form a rooted spanning tree; each merger, reconnection, shared
tail, cycle, seam, reflection, or wrapping edge is a uniquely owned closure edge. The record reconstructs
the occupied geometric network without duplicating shared continuations.

Proof. Vacuum factorization follows by deleting the reported physical connectors and applying Fubini
across every trivial compact cut. Otherwise the least mixed cut, least nontrivial representation label, and
least oriented half-edge choose the root and first edge. Deterministic depth-first exploration then classifies
every occupied unoriented edge exactly once as a discovery or closure edge. A composite singlet is retained
whenever any internal separating cut is nontrivial, independently of the visible output label.

Lemma 6.28 (Product Fourier–Wiener calculus). For scalar f ∈ AI , convolution by f on L2 (GI ) is
trace class and
                                ∥Kf ∥S1 (L2 (GI )) = ∥f ∥AI .                               (63)
If I ⊂ I ′ , lifting f by independence of the coordinates in I ′ \ I preserves the norm. Pointwise multiplication
satisfies
                                              ∥f g∥AI ≤ ∥f ∥AI ∥g∥AI .                                       (64)
The same inequalities hold coefficientwise in the declared finite projective marked norm. Left/right
translations, inversion, and coordinate permutations are isometries.
Proof. Peter–Weyl decomposes L2 (GI ) into the blocks Vλ ⊗ Vλ∗ . Convolution has block fb(λ) ⊗ I, whose
trace norm is dλ ∥fb(λ)∥S1 ; summing gives (63). A lifted function has only the trivial Fourier block in
every added coordinate, proving isometry. The standard coefficient-product formula, complete contraction
in the unitary Clebsch–Gordan maps, and the projective trace norm give (64). The remaining symmetries
are unitary changes of Peter–Weyl blocks.

a fixed polynomial in u−1 , while the denominator is the trial-ball lower bound [c0 ud0 /2 e−CRg ]|X| ; the

variables are integrated. Cross-core words remain in the explicit connected row. Summing (HT3) proves
the collar/irregular score row.
   Higher pivot derivatives are obtained by differentiating the normalized-score identity. They are finite
Bell polynomials in derivatives of the complete local action, Jacobian, and normalized cumulants. The
fixed-group atlas and the C ∞ splice templates supply these derivatives through order 2sG ; the plateau
denominator and differentiated total-variation bounds control each normalized cumulant without a
core-volume factor. Thus the hth derivative costs only a fixed polynomial in u−1 .
   Because u ranges in the fixed compact interval I⋆ , all remaining finite powers of u−1 are absorbed into
the displayed CG,h (1 + u−h ) hierarchy. A finite atlas covers the one-pivot compact fiber, so the score
constants are uniform in U , exterior data, volume, and preceding weak depth. Integrating the first score
along a geodesic of length at most diam G proves the oscillation bound. Marking one fixed factor uses
the corresponding marked rows.
   For a finite family of active straight macro-paths, keep every fine link on those active paths in its
original link coordinate. An active path carries one nontrivial retained Peter–Weyl coefficient, or one
bounded scalar probe whose first derivative at the origin is centered. Choose live pivots P = {p1 , . . . , pN }
among the original links of the active paths, with mutually disjoint buffered neighborhoods separated
by distance R and separated from all path endpoints. Condition on every nonpivot fine link, every link
outside the active paths, and all exterior/source data; denote this collection by η. Do not condition on a
retained path product carrying an active coefficient or probe.
   Assign every physical factor by its incidence in the original fine-link support: factors meeting no live
pivot enter H0 , complete owned factors meeting exactly pi enter the ith one-pivot weight, and connected
factors meeting at least two live pivots enter the multipivot remainder. The active retained carriers are
distinguished insertions and are never absorbed into a one-pivot density or counted as physical activities.
   Define

              Ω(u) := CΩ (ū)(u−1 + 1),                                                                    (45)

where cp > 0 is the original-support separation constant. The constant CΩ (ū) may depend on the fixed
crossing interval I⋆ , equivalently on its positive lower endpoint ū; downstream arguments consume only
the finiteness of Ω⋆ , never uniformity as ū ↓ 0.

only an U -independent scalar. For the multipivot row, every activity is reported in its original connected
fine-link support. Split it by the three species in Proposition 6.8. A reference factor meeting separated
pivots has diam A ≥ cp R(|IP (A)| − 1); a regular or irregular connected factor has |A| ≥ cp R(|IP (A)| − 1).

by the pivot charge eλ|IP (A)| , the reserved exponent absorbs that charge for sufficiently large finite R
and yields exactly the three terms of ∆R (u). Thus no diameter estimate is used to pay a spanning-tree
weight. Coordinate substitutions on inactive paths are independent of all live pivots and cannot create
additional pivot incidence. Physical-source marks of order at most two change only Cλ ; the independent
pivot derivatives through order 2sG are already included in the three norms in (HT5).
   Finally, on an active path no anchor is reconstructed: all path links remain original variables. Splitting
the ordered path product immediately gives (49). Haar invariance therefore annihilates the Haar part of
every nontrivial carrier at an uncovered pivot under the same integral in which the product reference was
obtained.

Proof. Before any path-product coordinate is introduced, the Wilson base measure is the product of
normalized Haar measures on the original fine links. Conditioning on all nonpivot links therefore leaves
one exact
       P Haar factor for each live pivot. Fubini and exact ownership split the physical interaction into
H0 + i hi + V≥2 and give (47). The active carrier is not part of the physical interaction, so it remains a
distinguished insertion.
   The one-pivot oscillation is precisely the complete one-pivot ledger proved above; normalization changes
only an U -independent scalar. For the multipivot row, every activity is reported in its original connected
fine-link support. Split it by the three species in Proposition 6.8. A reference factor meeting separated
pivots has diam A ≥ cp R(|IP (A)| − 1); a regular or irregular connected factor has |A| ≥ cp R(|IP (A)| − 1).
Reserve respectively mref − a′ref , areg − a′reg , and airr − a′irr in the three input rows. After multiplying
by the pivot charge eλ|IP (A)| , the reserved exponent absorbs that charge for sufficiently large finite R
and yields exactly the three terms of ∆R (u). Thus no diameter estimate is used to pay a spanning-tree
weight. Coordinate substitutions on inactive paths are independent of all live pivots and cannot create
additional pivot incidence. Physical-source marks of order at most two change only Cλ ; the independent
pivot derivatives through order 2sG are already included in the three norms in (HT5).
   Finally, on an active path no anchor is reconstructed: all path links remain original variables. Splitting
the ordered path product immediately gives (49). Haar invariance therefore annihilates the Haar part of
every nontrivial carrier at an uncovered pivot under the same integral in which the product reference was
obtained.

                                                        Λ



The compact transfer operator has a simple maximal eigenvalue and a unique strictly positive Perron
eigenfunction. No volume-uniform lower bound for the complete kernel is asserted.
Proof. Apply Theorem 6.10 with one live pivot at a time. Corollary 6.11 gives the finite-volume product
lower bound. The aligned half-step is SL∗ SL , so the transfer is positive and self-adjoint. Jentzsch–Perron
applies in finite volume.

6.5    Exact Haar–optical matching law
The matching macroblock used after the compact first crossing is the exact pushforward by oriented
length-L path products. It is useful to distinguish the coordinates that define the map from the coordinates
used to estimate a particular retained coefficient.
   Fix one proof color. Its straight macro-paths are pairwise edge-disjoint and have mutually disjoint
buffered interiors. For one path γ = (e1 , . . . , eL ) define
                                            Pγ (U ) = Ue1 · · · UeL .
The retained macro-link is Wγ = Pγ (U ). For paths not carrying an active coefficient one may use the
standard coordinates
                            Wγ = Pγ (U ),      Zγ,k = Uek , 2 ≤ k ≤ L,
with
                                         Ue1 = Wγ (Zγ,2 · · · Zγ,L )−1 .
The full map BL is the finite composition of the colorwise pushforwards, with the reflection plane on
macro-cell boundaries.
Lemma 6.14 (Unit-Haar macroblock disintegration). For every path,
                                L
                                Y                              L
                                                               Y
                                      dmG (Uek ) = dmG (Wγ )         dmG (Zγ,k ).
                                k=1                            k=2

Consequently the simultaneous path-product map has unit Haar Jacobian, is gauge covariant at path
endpoints, and intertwines aligned reflection.
Proof. For fixed details, Ue1 7→ Wγ is right multiplication by a fixed group element. Haar invariance
proves the identity. Edge-disjointness tensorizes it, gauge transformations telescope, and reflection reverses
and inverts the product.
Definition 6.15 (Carrier-adapted hybrid chart). Let A be the finite set of retained macro-links carrying
nontrivial Peter–Weyl labels, bounded probes, or retained-source derivatives in the coefficient under study.
On every path γ ∈ A retain the original link coordinates
                                         Xγ,k = Uek ,        1 ≤ k ≤ L,
and regard Wγ = Pγ (X) only as the output map. On paths γ ∈        / A use the standard (Wγ , Zγ,2:L )
coordinates. Choose live pivots only among the X variables on active paths.

Corollary 6.19 (Definition, exactness, and coefficientwise use of BL ). The matching macroblock BL is
a well-defined finite exact pushforward on the compact first-crossing class. For every Fourier, source-
marked, or normalized-probe coefficient of its retained density, the active paths may be analyzed in the
carrier-adapted hybrid chart without changing that coefficient. The charged pivot expansion uses precisely
the reference of Lemma 6.18; gauge covariance, reflection, and dyadic composition remain properties of
the underlying exact pushforward.
6.5.1 Compact collect with optical field live
The matching theorem is now proved on one exact joint law, before the normal/optical variables are
marginalized. This is the decisive ordering. The compact retained variables are reduced conditionally by
Haar–pivot integration; the resulting activities remain functions of the complete optical field; and only
then is the connected logarithm taken by the marked Gaussian forest theorem. Thus no retained density,
latent mixture, or residual-only gas is inserted between the physical law and the connected source kernel.



                                                       59
6.5.2 One exact joint matching law
Let W denote the compact retained variables in the exact Karcher chart and let ζ denote its complete
optical normal field. At finite volume and with physical source J, Theorem 4.13, Theorem 3.28, and the
exact owner map give
                              dµJ (W, ζ) = Z(J)−1 dγopt (ζ) dH(W ) e−VJ (W,ζ) .                   (53)
Here dH(W ) is product normalized Haar on the compact retained links. Indeed, ρ0,j in Theorem 4.13 owns
only the field-independent linear Jacobian and empty-support normalization; Proposition 6.8 absorbs that
scalar into Z(J) and restores product Haar. Every nonempty retained reference factor remains explicit in
VJ . Every nonempty Haar, coarea, chart, determinant, Schur, regular, irregular, seam, wrapping, and
source occurrence belongs to exactly one factor of VJ . Every scalar depending on an unintegrated variable
remains inside (53). Define only the complete normalized source logarithm

                                          W(J) = log Z(J) − log Z(0).                                   (54)

No marginal density is logarithmized.
Lemma 6.20 (Exact Haar projection contraction). Let Pp denote Haar averaging in one live pivot
variable and Qp = I − Pp . For every complete one-pivot reference of Theorem 6.10, Corollary 6.11 gives
the exact decomposition

                         νp,η = ϵ⋆ mG + θ⋆ νep,η ,       0 < ϵ⋆ < 1,      θ⋆ = 1 − ϵ⋆ < 1.

This is the complete nonvacuum projection estimate, not a coefficientwise claim. It is group-generic.
Source marks are inserted later as marked vertices and may remove at most one unmarked payment per
mark.

                                           O       e−hi (X;η)
                                 νη(0) =                              dmG (X)
                                                 e−hi (Y ;η) dmG (Y )
                                               R
                                           i   G

is exact. Every active retained carrier remains a function of the original active-path links and varies
through each uncovered pivot as in (51). In particular, the product reference and the Haar-through-carrier
cancellation belong to one and the same conditional integral.
Proof. Apply Lemma 6.16, then split and normalize the physical factors by exact ownership. The active
retained coordinates were never conditioned and no anchor was reconstructed on their paths. Lemma 6.17
gives the final statement.
Corollary 6.19 (Definition, exactness, and coefficientwise use of BL ). The matching macroblock BL is
a well-defined finite exact pushforward on the compact first-crossing class. For every Fourier, source-
marked, or normalized-probe coefficient of its retained density, the active paths may be analyzed in the
carrier-adapted hybrid chart without changing that coefficient. The charged pivot expansion uses precisely
the reference of Lemma 6.18; gauge covariance, reflection, and dyadic composition remain properties of
the underlying exact pushforward.
6.5.1 Compact collect with optical field live
The matching theorem is now proved on one exact joint law, before the normal/optical variables are
marginalized. This is the decisive ordering. The compact retained variables are reduced conditionally by
Haar–pivot integration; the resulting activities remain functions of the complete optical field; and only
then is the connected logarithm taken by the marked Gaussian forest theorem. Thus no retained density,
latent mixture, or residual-only gas is inserted between the physical law and the connected source kernel.



                                                       59
6.5.2 One exact joint matching law
Let W denote the compact retained variables in the exact Karcher chart and let ζ denote its complete
optical normal field. At finite volume and with physical source J, Theorem 4.13, Theorem 3.28, and the
exact owner map give
                              dµJ (W, ζ) = Z(J)−1 dγopt (ζ) dH(W ) e−VJ (W,ζ) .                   (53)
Here dH(W ) is product normalized Haar on the compact retained links. Indeed, ρ0,j in Theorem 4.13 owns
only the field-independent linear Jacobian and empty-support normalization; Proposition 6.8 absorbs that
scalar into Z(J) and restores product Haar. Every nonempty retained reference factor remains explicit in
VJ . Every nonempty Haar, coarea, chart, determinant, Schur, regular, irregular, seam, wrapping, and
source occurrence belongs to exactly one factor of VJ . Every scalar depending on an unintegrated variable
remains inside (53). Define only the complete normalized source logarithm

                                          W(J) = log Z(J) − log Z(0).                                   (54)

No marginal density is logarithmized.
Lemma 6.20 (Exact Haar projection contraction). Let Pp denote Haar averaging in one live pivot
variable and Qp = I − Pp . For every complete one-pivot reference of Theorem 6.10, Corollary 6.11 gives
the exact decomposition

                         νp,η = ϵ⋆ mG + θ⋆ νep,η ,       0 < ϵ⋆ < 1,      θ⋆ = 1 − ϵ⋆ < 1.

Consequently its convolution/conditional-expectation operator satisfies

                        max ∥Tp,η Qp ∥L∞ →L∞ , ∥Tp,η Qp ∥L2 (mG )→L2 (mG ) ≤ θ⋆ .                       (55)
                             


This is the complete nonvacuum projection estimate, not a coefficientwise claim. It is group-generic.
Source marks are inserted later as marked vertices and may remove at most one unmarked payment per
mark.

Proof. For Pp f = 0,
                                     Tp,η f = ϵ⋆ Pp f + θ⋆ Te
                                                            νp,η
                                                                 f = θ⋆ Te
                                                                         νp,η
                                                                              f.
Translation by the fixed left and right carrier factors is an isometry in both norms. The marked statement
is exactly the one/two-mark ownership row of Theorem 6.10.

6.5.3 Final compact Haar-pivot collect
In (53), write U for the compact matching links and keep the complete optical field ζ and the actual
local source roots unchanged. Apply the exact compact-only path-product block BL to U as a finite
matching operation. Corollary 6.19 permits coefficientwise use of its hybrid chart, while every compact
path detail is integrated in the same complete joint partition function. No optical variable is identified
with a compact path detail, and no source is assumed fiber-constant under BL . The block is used once to
place many same-law Haar pivots inside each coarse compact edge.
Definition 6.21 (Matching-stage tri-graded norm). For a compact-stage activity family F = (FX ) and
fixed positive mark weights σpiv , σζ , σJ , set
                                                                  |α|

=== hit 8 offset 244863 ===

ct transfer operator has a simple maximal eigenvalue and a unique strictly positive Perron
eigenfunction. No volume-uniform lower bound for the complete kernel is asserted.
Proof. Apply Theorem 6.10 with one live pivot at a time. Corollary 6.11 gives the finite-volume product
lower bound. The aligned half-step is SL∗ SL , so the transfer is positive and self-adjoint. Jentzsch–Perron
applies in finite volume.

6.5    Exact Haar–optical matching law
The matching macroblock used after the compact first crossing is the exact pushforward by oriented
length-L path products. It is useful to distinguish the coordinates that define the map from the coordinates
used to estimate a particular retained coefficient.
   Fix one proof color. Its straight macro-paths are pairwise edge-disjoint and have mutually disjoint
buffered interiors. For one path γ = (e1 , . . . , eL ) define
                                            Pγ (U ) = Ue1 · · · UeL .
The retained macro-link is Wγ = Pγ (U ). For paths not carrying an active coefficient one may use the
standard coordinates
                            Wγ = Pγ (U ),      Zγ,k = Uek , 2 ≤ k ≤ L,
with
                                         Ue1 = Wγ (Zγ,2 · · · Zγ,L )−1 .
The full map BL is the finite composition of the colorwise pushforwards, with the reflection plane on
macro-cell boundaries.
Lemma 6.14 (Unit-Haar macroblock disintegration). For every path,
                                L
                                Y                              L
                                                               Y
                                      dmG (Uek ) = dmG (Wγ )         dmG (Zγ,k ).
                                k=1                            k=2

Consequently the simultaneous path-product map has unit Haar Jacobian, is gauge covariant at path
endpoints, and intertwines aligned reflection.
Proof. For fixed details, Ue1 7→ Wγ is right multiplication by a fixed group element. Haar invariance
proves the identity. Edge-disjointness tensorizes it, gauge transformations telescope, and reflection reverses
and inverts the product.
Definition 6.15 (Carrier-adapted hybrid chart). Let A be the finite set of retained macro-links carrying
nontrivial Peter–Weyl labels, bounded probes, or retained-source derivatives in the coefficient under study.
On every path γ ∈ A retain the original link coordinates
                                         Xγ,k = Uek ,        1 ≤ k ≤ L,
and regard Wγ = Pγ (X) only as the output map. On paths γ ∈        / A use the standard (Wγ , Zγ,2:L )
coordinates. Choose live pivots only among the X variables on active paths.

                  γ e∈γ              γ∈A e∈γ                γ ∈A
                                                              /            k=2

Conditioning on all variables except separated live pivots on the active paths leaves the exact product Haar
law on those pivots. No active retained product is conditioned.
Proof. Use the identity of Lemma 6.14 only on inactive paths and the identity map on active paths.
Same-color paths are edge-disjoint, so the factors tensorize. The final conditional statement is then
immediate from (50).

Lemma 6.17 (Carrier incidence and original-support locality). Let p be a live pivot on an active path γ.
With all other active path links fixed,
                                        Wγ = Bp Xp±1 Cp ,                                           (51)
where Bp , Cp are independent of Xp . If a connected physical activity with original reported support Y
meets k separated live pivots, then
                                          τ (Y ) ≥ cp R(k − 1).                                     (52)
Anchor reconstruction on an inactive path cannot alter the activity’s live pivot incidence.
Proof. Equation (51) is the ordered product split before and after p. The second statement follows by
joining the k pivot buffers inside the connected reported support. Every inactive-path coordinate is a
function only of links on that inactive path; because no live pivot lies there, its substitution cannot create
dependence on a live pivot.

    For a tree carrying two distinguished source buffers, let v(A, B) be their unique lowest common ancestor.
If v(A, B) belongs to the terminal matching logarithm, call the history a matching history. Otherwise its
scale label j(v) is the unique weak coarsest-connection scale. Notice that this is a property of the exact
recursively generated tree, not a choice made after absolute values or bounds have been applied.
Corollary 8.7 (No omission and no double counting). The connected two-buffer part of D2 Wm (0) is
the disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw .
Contracting all strict descendants of v(A, B) produces a one/two-root decorated vertex whose norm is
bounded uniformly by Theorem 7.2 and Corollary 7.4. The decomposition remains exact after regular and
irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two
source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6.
Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by
its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every
scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure.

root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.

4.3    Gaussian reference and optical covariance
This section constructs the Gaussian objects used by the weak block, Wick coordinates, replica forests,
and source connectors. Two objects must not be conflated:
1. the fixed tangent Feynman Gaussian on mean-zero lattice one-forms;
2. its exact conditional detail Gaussian for the linearized forty-nine-path Karcher block.
The first carries the massless retained field. The second is an optical, high-pass covariance with a uniform
gap. The nonlinear Wilson action, Haar and exponential-coordinate density, Karcher coarea factor, cutoffs,
inherited interaction, and source factors are not hidden in either covariance; they are exposed as exact
activities after the normal-fiber change of variables.
