                          b                                                b
                                    Y ∋b                                        Y ∋b
                               τL (Y )>R(L)

                                                        ≤ Cm LΓ e−κR(L) ≤ Cm L−3 .

   For the short sector, first apply exact collect and then the local projector. Linearity and absolute
convergence make this identical to localizing each history and collecting by its canonical hull. Every
stable remainder lies in the kernel of LocD , while every off-diagonal local/remainder block has the same
Taylor-remainder gain. Since
                                              τL (Y ) ≤ R(L),
the discrete covariant localization lemma gives

                                              CD,m (1 + R(L))pD L3−D

per collected activity. Summing the rooted envelope contributes LΓ , proving the short bound.
   The canonical root and history certificate are selected once, so neither hull reassignment nor collect
produces a volume factor. Uniformity follows from the rooted envelope and the finite local template
atlas.

Boundaries and aligned reflection. Use the corrected reflection schedule:
• block the two closed half-spaces separately;
• retain the reflection-plane link layer as fixed boundary data;
• use only path templates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

The six one-step maps.          The exact regular step is organized in the following order.
(E1) Normal chart return. Substitute the finite-volume analytic normal section into the local Wilson,
     Haar, inherited, and source factors.


                                                              34
(E2) Coarea logarithm. Append the field-dependent logarithm

                                                  − log det(I + DN P ),

     with the fixed linear determinant owned once outside the activity map.
(E3) Summed connected logarithm. Apply the simultaneous hard-core/Gaussian replica-BKAR
     identity and sum the exact 1/n! series.
(E4) Local projection. Extract the complete gauge-covariant Wick-local jet and retain the short Taylor
     remainder.
(E5) Canonical hull and collect. Report covariance paths and finite collars, assign each history to
     one canonical hull, and collect exactly once.
(E6) Output recertification. Derive the next regulator allocation from the actual post-localization
     output gradient and complex Hessian.

Permanent support exponent.
Lemma 4.24 (One common support exponent). Suppose the covariance connector has decay exponent
µ > 0, and the normal chart, inverse trees, coarea logarithm, and fixed local atlas have positive exponential
locality exponents
                                      mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas > 0.
Choose
                                0 < κ < min{µ, mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas }.
Then all six maps act in the same κ-weighted rooted support space. No per-step weakening of κ is required.

Proof. For a BKAR tree, report every covariance edge by a shortest path and every hard-core edge by its
fixed collar. The geometric reporting inequality gives
                                        X           X
                               τ (P ) ≤   τ (Si ) +    de + Cgeo (n − 1).
                                           i            e∈Tcov


Multiplication by eκτ (P ) leaves the residual connector decay e−(µ−κ)de . Every fixed chart, inverse, coarea,
and atlas branch is summable at exponent κ. Localization costs a polynomial in the rooted radius but
does not alter the exponent. Canonical collect is a partition of histories, so it does not duplicate a support
weight.

                                                 ∥A∥phys ≤ u1/2−ϵ .

                                       ∥Hi ∥phys ≤ u1/2−ϵ ,       1 ≤ i ≤ r.                            (SN)

marked row below is a derivative in an unscaled physical unit direction.
Lemma 4.25 (One common analytic species tube). There is a radius ηcom > 0, independent of volume
and preceding weak depth, on which the Wilson word, exponential-coordinate Haar density, Karcher
normal return, coarea logarithm, and finite Schur endpoint are simultaneously holomorphic. On the closed
half-radius tube, their derivatives through structural order three and every fixed passive-source order have
finite fixed-template suprema.

Proof. Each species is a finite composition of multiplication, inversion, exponential, logarithm, the
finite-cell Karcher mean, and an inverse of one of the matrices

                                         I + DN P,          Hopt + B(A).


                                                        35
The path-atlas theorem supplies a positive holomorphy radius for the first five maps. The uniform
inequalities ∥DN P ∥ < 1 and Hopt ≥ 2I persist on a smaller positive tube, so the two inverses are
holomorphic there by a Neumann series. There are only finitely many bulk and aligned-reflection
templates. Taking the minimum of their radii gives ηcom > 0; Cauchy’s estimate on the closed half-radius
tube gives the uniform derivative constants.
   For each fixed local template define the finite constants CW , CHaar , Ccoa , Cchart , CSch as the suprema
on that closed common tube of the third derivatives of respectively the Wilson word, Haar logarithm,
coarea logarithm, normal return, and finite Schur endpoint after their scalar and complete two-jet parts
have been removed. Let Cinc be the largest rooted incidence number of those templates and let Cconn be
the row sum of the exponential covariance connector of Corollary 4.14. All these constants are finite and
uniform under the standing convention.

                         1                                              3

                                    ηatom (u, r, b) ≤ Catom (uω0 + r + b),
with the same estimate after three structural marks and every fixed passive source order.

5       Invariant graph and universal coefficient
The marginal coordinate is u = g 2 . Inputs SI–6, SI–6B, SI–7, and SI–7M are used only to compute
the local perturbative Taylor coefficient of the complete fine Wilson vector-minus-ghost quotient. The
coefficient of the exact Karcher map is identified internally by evaluating one local standard background
coordinate on the two equal measures related by the exact Karcher pushforward, followed by unit-tangent
conjugacy on the centered invariant family. No unrestricted Haar-translated background functional, global
background-gauge section, or application of Reisz power counting to the optical conditional covariance is
consumed.
   In the canonical squared-coupling normalization,

The factor log 2 is the physical scale-two interval. The complete coordinate-intertwining theorem is
printed after the centered invariant graph, where exact measure equality and parabolic conjugacy identify
the one-step coefficient.

6.5.3 Final compact Haar-pivot collect
In (53), write U for the compact matching links and keep the complete optical field ζ and the actual
local source roots unchanged. Apply the exact compact-only path-product block BL to U as a finite
matching operation. Corollary 6.19 permits coefficientwise use of its hybrid chart, while every compact
path detail is integrated in the same complete joint partition function. No optical variable is identified
with a compact path detail, and no source is assumed fiber-constant under BL . The block is used once to
place many same-law Haar pivots inside each coarse compact edge.

Lemma 6.23 (Tri-graded optical/source upgrade). In the exact factorization of Theorem 6.10, the
normalized one-pivot logarithmic scores, VA,J , zA,J = e−VA,J − 1, the exact positive denominators, and

Proof. Differentiate the exact compact factorization before integrating the optical field. At this call site ζ

    For the reference, regular, coarea, determinant, and Gaussian-normalization factors, apply Lemma 6.22

with |α| ≤ 2sG , r ≤ 3, and q ≤ 2. Transition and outer labels are finite-dimensional smooth densities on

the component size and is absorbed by the bad-action exponential.
   The maps V 7→ e−V − 1, multiplication, inversion of the strictly positive scalar denominator, and
the normalized logarithmic-score map are analytic maps of this finite-order tri-graded Banach algebra.
Leibniz, Faà di Bruno, and the quotient rule are paid by the three factorial weights in (56). Since G
is fixed, the derivative orders 2sG , three, and two are fixed finite integers, so the resulting constant is
uniform in all declared limits. This proves the stated tri-graded row without identifying an optical field
derivative with a passive source derivative.

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

Here A G is the exact Gaussian cross-core inventory of Theorem 3.28, A reg and A irr are the exact
owner-map outputs, and A H is the nonvacuum compact inventory of Theorem 6.39. The scalar Zloc (J)
is formed only after every variable owned by a one-core reference has been integrated. Every physical,
normalization, seam, wrapping, determinant, full-incidence compact-vacuum, and source occurrence is
present exactly once in either a one-core reference or one of the four disjoint cross-core inventories; every

Proof. Start from (53). Apply the exact Gaussian product identity of Theorem 3.28, the canonical owner
map, and Theorem 6.39. Convert each connected Gaussian or logarithmic cross-core word to its exact
Mayer factor e−GA − 1. Expand first over arbitrary finite subsets, including overlapping supports. Collect
each connected overlap component onto its least canonical reported hull; the resulting hull activities have
disjoint exact histories. Only after this overlap collect is performed are the remaining hull families declared
compatible. History compatibility therefore makes the four ownership classes disjoint and exhaustive
without omitting an overlapping Mayer term. Fubini gives (100); no scalar depending on an unintegrated
variable enters Zloc .

the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every

length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

Theorem 7.2 (Retained probe-root influence row). For every bounded centered probe in a fixed matching-
scale source buffer, the exact normalized response satisfies the zero-, one-, and two-source weighted row

Theorem 7.3 (Exact local-source retention and Gate–E closure). Let F be a bounded local matching
observable and let B be any bounded registered exterior perturbation supported at distance at least r from
F . Through source order two,

                             DJq ⟨F ; B⟩c J=0 ≤ CF,B,q e−mE r ,     q = 0, 1, 2.                      (107)

The same estimate holds for a finite family of compact-boundary or optical chart perturbations whose
centered score rows are uniformly bounded in the permanent marked norm. This is the boundary-to-center
statement consumed by the source forest.
Proof. Use F and B as the two declared roots in Theorem 6.43. The owner inventory is exhaustive at the
vertex level, and Corollary 7.8 excludes an additional unreported acoustic class. For a finite interpolation
of compact boundary data, differentiation along a unit-speed group geodesic inserts its centered local
score as the exterior root; for an admitted optical chart interpolation the affine derivative inserts the
corresponding registered optical score. The marked row is uniform in the exterior data allowed by the
exact regular/irregular owner decomposition. Integrating the finite interpolation gives the same estimate.
No claim for arbitrary uncontrolled boundary measures is needed downstream.
Corollary 7.4 (Uniform two-buffer connector rows). Let I1 , I2 be bounded source, displacement, stress,
or retained-link buffers. Every exact connected response meeting both buffers has exponentially weighted

7.1    Acoustic exclusion at the exact matching call site
Lemma 7.5 (No massless retained reference at matching). In the exact post-compact joint partition of
Corollary 6.40, the retained compact field has product normalized Haar reference and has already been
integrated coefficientwise before the optical connected logarithm is taken. The massless tangent Feynman
Gaussian used in the weak normal-chart construction is not a live matching reference. The only Gaussian
reference left live is the normalized optical one-core product, and every cross-core optical term is contained
in the explicit inventory AJG .
Proof. The exact compact collect of Theorem 6.39 and the post-compact identity of Corollary 6.40 restore
product Haar on the retained compact variables and place every nonempty retained dependence into the
compact, Gaussian, regular, or irregular activity inventories. The weak tangent Gaussian contributes
no undeclared covariance edge to this matching logarithm. By contrast, the optical one-core references
remain live, while their off-diagonal quadratic blocks and other cross-core terms occur explicitly in AJG
and retain the spatial payment used in Theorem 6.43.
Lemma 7.6 (Vacuum factorization after the complete owner map). Fix the optical variables and expand
one compact coefficient in Peter–Weyl sectors. Suppose every compact cut separating two declared root
buffers carries the trivial representation. Remove only the cross-core objects already registered in the
four disjoint inventories of Corollary 6.40. If no registered connector remains between the roots, the

preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

buffers carries the trivial representation. Remove only the cross-core objects already registered in the
four disjoint inventories of Corollary 6.40. If no registered connector remains between the roots, the

Proof. Triviality on every separating compact cut permits repeated Haar–Fubini separation. The exact

Theorem 7.7 (Exact acoustic exclusion-or-capture). Consider any connected zero-, one-, or two-source
coefficient joining two bounded matching-scale root buffers in the exact joint logarithm. At least one object
on the unique root-to-root forest path belongs to one of the following classes:
   (i) a nonvacuum compact activity controlled by Theorem 6.39 and the spatial row (99);
  (ii) an optical covariance bond or an optical Gaussian cross-core activity in AJG ;
 (iii) a registered reference, regular, irregular, seam, reflection, wrapping, normalization, determinant, or
       source connector with its original support payment.
There is no fourth class consisting of a massless retained acoustic reference edge.
Proof. Assume that a connected coefficient contains no object of types (i)–(iii). Absence of (i) makes
every separating compact cut vacuum. Absence of (iii) leaves no registered physical connector after the
complete owner map. Lemma 7.6 then reduces the compact contribution to disconnected one-core factors
and scalars. Absence of (ii) also prevents the still-live optical field from joining the roots. The coefficient
is therefore disconnected, a contradiction. The only possible remaining concern would be an undeclared
massless retained covariance, which is excluded by Lemma 7.5.

Fix ε > 0. Then

Theorem 9.3 (Unique vacuum and complete local mass gap). The OS Hamiltonian satisfies

                                               H Ω⊥ ≥ m > 0.                                          (169)

The vacuum is unique.
Proof. The centered classes [F ] − S(F )Ω, F ∈ D+ , are dense in Ω⊥ . Lemma 9.2 says that the spectral
projection EH ([0, m − ε]) annihilates this dense set for every ε > 0; hence it vanishes on Ω⊥ . This proves
the absence of spectrum in (0, m).
   For a centered core vector, (168) tends to zero, so its spectral measure has no atom at zero. Density
then shows that EH ({0})H = CΩ. Thus the vacuum is unique and (169) holds.

10     Nontriviality and finite spectral window
10.1     Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
Let Yr (f ) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
positive Euclidean time.
Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which

                                    V0 (f ) := S0 ΘYr (f ) Yr (f ) > 0.
                                                                  


The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

10.2     Uniform endpoint interpolation
Theorem 10.2 (Uniform endpoint interpolation master theorem). Fix the positive-time profile f and a
                                                   √
physical weak scale r, put ur = u(r) and gr = ur , and use g rather than u = g 2 as the interpolation
parameter. For the complete finite-volume normalized reflected two-root functional, and also with one
fixed finite defect root, the following hold on the full segment 0 ≤ g ≤ gr :
    (i) the graph-segment identities are C 2 and exact before every limit;
   (ii) the stable/wrapping orbit rows, the pre-owner two-mark compact entrance row, and the terminal
        packet-shell row have constants independent of cutoff, volume, preceding weak depth, crossing cell,
        and terminal depth M ;
  (iii) differentiation is performed inside each fixed crossing cell before ownership, never on the crossing
        index, and the resulting total derivatives extend across cell boundaries;
  (iv) the no-crossing terminal remainder tends to zero through two g-derivatives uniformly as M → ∞.

Consequently there is one finite Cf , depending only on the fixed profile and finite source order, such that

Lemma 10.3 (Existence of the graph-segment interpolation). After choosing r in the common weak
corridor, the segment 0 ≤ g ≤ gr lies in the same real marked domain. At finite cutoff and volume,
evaluate the exact interaction at (g 2 , R⋆ (g 2 )) and express its quadratic part relative to the fixed g = 0
grounded Gaussian. This gives a real C 2 family Vg,r with positive finite normalization. In fixed Gaussian
coordinates its complete local expansion has the exact grading
                           X
                   Vg,r =      g ν Vn,ν;r ,     ν ≥ max{n − 2, 0},       ν ≡ n (mod 2).                   (171)
                            n,ν

Here n is field degree. The Wilson row attains the lower exponent ν = n − 2; Haar, chart, coarea,
determinant, and graph-return rows carry additional nonnegative powers. The grading is read only after
each complete BRS/reflection packet is collected. The graph is C 3 in u, so g 7→ (g 2 , R⋆ (g 2 )) is C 2 at
g = 0. The Wilson word, Haar density, normal return, coarea, determinant, and finite Schur endpoint are
analytic in the physical field A = ga. After the Gaussian quadratic form is removed, a Wilson term of field
degree n carries g n−2 ; species without the g −2 Wilson prefactor carry higher powers. Analyticity under
A = ga and the even dependence of the graph coefficients on g give the congruence in (171). Gauge/ghost
partners and coordinate Jacobians are collected before the grading is read, so BRS and reflection relations
are retained. No C 1 statement in the squared coupling u is used. Finite-volume Gaussian equivalence and
the common analytic tube give positivity, integrability, and uniform C 2 control in g.

Lemma A.12 (Top marked ancestor and complete-packet shell). In a packet tree from Lemma A.11,
contract every maximal descendant subtree carrying no distinguished label into its exact parent-normalized
decoration, and suppress every maximal unary chain into its exact ordered transfer product. The distin-
guished labels are the two curvature roots and the zero, one or two active g marks. They have a unique
lowest common ancestor v⊤ ; let j be its conditional scale. Call v⊤ the top marked ancestor.
    All vertices strictly above v⊤ are unmarked. The complete coefficient created at v⊤ includes every
contracted side decoration with its canonical reported support. Decompose these whole coefficients by their
full reported-support shell k using a scalar translation- and reflection-invariant selector. The selected
class is therefore a complete BRS/reflection packet with every denominator and normalization contact
still inside it.
    If j ≤ k + C0 , the permanent rooted-support row of that whole packet gives
                                                                 k−j
                                            C(1 + 2k−j )q e−c2         .                                   (208)

If j > k + C0 , the full support lies inside a fixed enlargement of one scale-j cell and no spatial connector
estimate is needed.

Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical
reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these
operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching
edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The
unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a
coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence
no marked vertex can occur above v⊤ .
   The shell selector is applied only after the full packet at v⊤ has been collected. A remote unmarked
side decoration is not cut from its parent and is not asked to carry an external Taylor factor. If exact
localization turns its contribution into a smaller-hull contact, that term is already in the finite contact block
and is not a shell-k packet. Otherwise its canonical reported support remains part of the whole packet.
When that support spans 2k−j scale-j blocks, the strict rooted-support norm and the weak connector
theorem give (208), including covariance, hard-core and mixed alternatives. The exact Loc/collect identity
of Theorem 5.8 ensures that this dichotomy is made before the stable packet is declared.
